115 年 8 月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
8 月實徵淨額 1,982 億元，較上年同月增加 445 億元(+28.9%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 3 兆 749 億元，較上年同期增加 6,191 億元
(+25.2%)。 
（三）達成率 
累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.0%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動 
（一）8 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以證券交易稅增加 262 億元、營業
稅、營利事業所得稅及綜合所得稅分別增加 68 億元、66 億元及 61
億元較多。 
（二）累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 2,855
億元 、營利事業所得稅增加 1,599 億元及營業稅增加 906 億元較多。 
圖 2 主要稅目別變化 
(115 年 1-8 月) 
1.營利事業所得稅 
(1)8 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 66 億元(+1.0 倍)，主因
大額退稅案件造成基期偏低所致。 
(2)累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 8,021 億元，較上年同期增加 1,599 億元
(+24.9%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加及延分期稅款陸續入帳。 
2.綜合所得稅 
8 月實徵淨額 890 億元，較上年同月增加 61 億元(+7.4%)，主因薪資
所得扣繳稅款增加；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 7,790 億元，較上年同期
增加 757 億元(+10.8%)，主因結算申報自繳及盈餘分配 、 薪資所得等
扣繳稅款俱增所致。 
3.營業稅 
(1)8月因資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額增加，營業稅
實徵淨額較上年同月增加68億元，由負125億元縮小為負56億元。 
(2)累計1至8月實徵淨額4,816億元 ， 較上年同期增加906億元(+23.2%)

### Page 3

3
，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額
增加，惟適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。
4.貨物稅
8月實徵淨額80億元，較上年同月減少43億元(-34.9%)，主因進口車
稅額減少及 汽柴油減徵幅度擴大 所致；累計1至8月實徵淨額 774億
元，較上年同期減少196億元(-20.2%)，除汽柴油降稅因素外，亦與
新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)8 月及累計 1 至 8 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 948 億
元、1 兆 1,606 億元，分別較上年同期(5,533 億元、4,247 億元)增
97.9%、1.7 倍。
(2)8 月 證 交稅 實徵 淨 額 540 億元 ，較 上 年同 月增加 262 億元
(+94.5%)；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 4,527 億元，較上年同期增加
2,855 億元(+1.7 倍)。
6.關稅
8 月實徵淨額 137 億元， 較上年同月增加9 億元(+7.4%)，累計 1 至 8

月實徵淨額 1,067 億元，較上年同期增加 77 億元(+7.8%)，均因機械
及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
8 月實徵淨額 32 億元，較上年同月增加 14 億元(+79.6%)，累計 1 至
8 月實徵淨額 188 億元，較上年同期增加 37 億元(+24.1%)，主因大
額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
8 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 10 億元(+22.7%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額 448 億元，較上年同期減少
26 億元(-5.5%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
（三）前 8 月主要徵起稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(273%)、遺產稅(159%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(128%)、 關稅(116%)、 營業稅(110%)、 契稅(110%)、 房屋稅(106%)、
綜所稅(102%)、營所稅(102%)及牌照稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(98%)、地價稅(88%)、土增稅

(85%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

### Page 4

4
三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 8 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 846 億元(占分配預算數 121.8%)，較上年同期增加 2,694 億元
(+14.8%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業 稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,128 億元(-90.8%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 2,855
億元、1,969 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 8 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵
淨額 9,117 億元(占分配預算
數 106.3%)，較上年同期增加
3,541 億元(+63.5%)， 主因納入
中央統籌分配 之營業稅 增加
3,113 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 8 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 174 億元，較
上年同期增加 3 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 61
億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-2.2%)；撥入長照基金 551 億元(其中 243

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、174 億元來自菸稅，133 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 33 億元(+6.3%)。

請參考末頁之免責宣言說明1

投資評等  強力買進

士電(1503)  
目標價位  250 元

獲利成長至 2028 年無虞

公司治理評鑑排序： 
1 2 3 4 5 6 7

個股基本資料：

徐培勛 
鋼鐵、重電產業 
S1120023@tss-c.com.tw 
 股價評價基礎： 
AI、半導體帶動用電需求激增，士電已接獲客戶需求，重電 T4 廠產能將於
4Q27 量產 ，2H28 開始貢獻營收 ；2027~2028 進入表後儲能工程認列高峰 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 稅後 淨利 YoY+29%/+33%/+15% ， 稅 後EPS 
8.10/10.80/12.46 元，給予強力買進評等，目標價 250 元(20x 2028 EPS)，
潛在漲幅 22%。

營運近況摘要： 
士電成立於 1955 年，為台灣老牌變壓器生產商，技術來自三菱重工的技轉，
1H26 產品組合：電力配電(變壓器、配電盤)72%、車輛零件 15%、自動化設
備(工具機)10%、其他 3%。內外銷比 65%：35%。客戶包含：Mitsubishi 
Electric/Mitsuba Electric/ Panasonic/ Hitachi ABB/ Siemens/泓德能源/起
而行/和泰車。目前三個廠區都在新竹：電裝廠、新豐廠及重電廠。

2026 年重電 T1+T2 廠產能利用率提升，T3 廠、表後儲能小幅貢獻 
預估 2026 年營收 415 億元(YoY+12%)，因變壓器需求殷切，重電 T1、T2
廠產能利用率提升至 90%，重電 T3 廠 2025/9 開始量產，Lead time 1~1.5
年，4Q26 開始認列營收，2027 年全面貢獻；且公司在手 500MW 表後儲能
(太陽能儲能)統包案也開始貢獻營收 ； 毛利率21%(YoY+0.2ppts)， 因產品組
合較同期相同； 業外則有 金融資產 公允價值、匯兌利益挹注，稅後淨利
YoY+29%，稅後 EPS 8.15 元。

2027~2028 年 T3、T4 廠產能提升，表後儲能進入認列高峰 
展望 2027~2028 年，重電內銷(佔重電 80%)：政府希望強韌電網計畫完工
期限從 2033 年提早至 2028 年，依 2027 年預算書依然維持 2033 年完工，
2026/2027/2028 資本支出 46/118/155 億元，維持成長態勢。重電外銷(佔
重電 20%)： 為了因應全球 AI、半導體等強勁用電需求，重電 T4 廠將於
2027/9 量產，預期2H28 開始貢獻營收，2029 年全面貢獻，T1+T2 年產能
10GVA(十億伏安)，T3 可貢獻產能 5GVA、T4 再貢獻 5GVA，T4 主要客戶
為日本、美國電力市場，並專注在 345KV 變壓器生產。國內表後儲能(太陽
能)：公司在手 500MW 表後儲能案，若假設 2026/2027/2028 完工比率
10%/40%/50%計算，預計貢獻 7.5/30/37.5 億元，

### Page 3

3 
證交所及櫃買中心公司治理評鑑級距如下：

一、 公司治理評鑑成績高者顯示其公司治理較符合主管機關要求，適合 1 年以上之長期投資或存股標的，與投資評等(強力買進等)僅 1
年內之投資建議不同。 
二、 依臺灣證券交易所及證券櫃檯買賣中心委託證券暨期貨市場發展基金會辦理的公司治理評鑑，每年將受評公司區分為 「上市公司」 、
「上櫃公司」 兩組，由70 項指標(1.維護股東權益及平等對待股東 11 項，占 16%，2.強化董事會結構與運作 18 項，占 25%，3.提
升資訊透明度 7 項，占 10%，4.推動永續發展 34 項，占 49%)編製而成，各組依評鑑成績高低分別公布七級距之公司名單，台新
投顧係引用民國 114(2025)年度之評鑑結果資料。

鑑結果僅顯示該企業之公司治理在本評鑑指標下之運作狀況，不宜過度仰賴，使用者必須自行衡量資訊的價值，主辦單位及執行單

Indicators Update 
2026 年 7 月機械受注 
製造業・非製造業（船電除く）のいずれも減少し、軟調な結果に

経済調査部 エコノミスト 吉井 希祐

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年7 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲3.7％と2 カ月ぶりに減少し、コン
センサス（Bloomberg 調査：同▲1.2％）を下回った。製造業は 2 カ月ぶりに減少し、
非製造業（船電除く）も 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持
ち直しの動きがみられる」に据え置いた。 
◼ 製造業からの受注額は前月比▲1.0％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。非鉄金属が前月に大型
案件があった反動で減少し、全体を下押しした。 非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額
は前月比▲2.6％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。通信業や情報サービス業、金融業・保険業
などの業種からの受注が減少した。 
◼ 先行きの民需（船電除く）は 緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。 企業の設備投資意
欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待され
よう。ただし、中東情勢の悪化に伴い、 受注が下振れするリスクには注意が必要だ 。
事業環境の不確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高騰・供給不足に対する懸念から、企業
の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある。

【総括】船電除く民需は2 カ月ぶりに減少 
2026 年 7 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲3.7％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少し、コンセン
サス（Bloomberg 調査：同▲1.2％）を下回った。製造業は2 カ月ぶりに減少し、非製造業（船
電除く）も2 カ月ぶりに減少した（図表 2）。民需（船電除く）を3 カ月移動平均で見ると同▲
2.6％となったが、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみられる」に据え置い
た。

【製造業】前月に大型案件があった非鉄金属が全体を下押し 
2026 年 7 月の製造業からの受注額は前月比▲1.0％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。機種別に見る
と、原動機・重電機、輸送機械が減少した一方、電子・通信機械、産業機械、工作機械は増加
した（図表 3 左、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、17 業種中 8 業種が減少した。非
鉄金属（同▲82.2％）が前月に大型案件があった反動で減少し、全体を下押しした。その他輸
送用機械（同▲41.4％）や化学工業（同▲12.0％）などからの受注も減少した。他方、電気機
械（同＋46.1％）や造船業（同＋88.9％）などからの受注は増加した。

【非製造業】通信業や情報サービス業などを中心に減少 
2026 年7 月の非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は前月比▲2.6％と2 カ月ぶりに減少した。
機種別に見ると、電子・通信機械、原動機・重電機 、産業機械、工作機械が減少した一方、輸
送機械は増加した（図表 3 右、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、11 業種中 6 業種が
減少した。 通信業（同▲ 23.2％）や情報サービス業（同▲ 16.9％） 、金融業・保険業（同 ▲
6.8％）などの業種からの受注が 減少した。他方、その他非製造業（同 ＋20.1％）や農林漁業
（同＋11.0％）などからの受注は増加した。

除外したが、26 年7 月は前月比▲7.8％であった。

【外需】前月に急増した反動などで2 カ月ぶりに減少 
2026 年 7 月の外需は前月比▲8.2％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した（図表 4）。前月に急増した反動
が表れたとみられる。機種別では、産業機械、電子・通信機械が減少した一方、原動機・重電
機、輸送機械、工作機械は増加した（図表 5、大和総研による季節調整値） 。原動機・重電機と
輸送機械ではそれぞれ3 件の大型案件があった。 
機械受注の外需動向を地域別に見る上で参考になる工作機械受注を確認すると、7 月の外需
は前月比＋1.4％と2 カ月連続で増加した（日本工作機械工業会、大和総研による季節調整値、
図表 6）。欧州（EU＋英国、同＋21.9％）からの受注は 2 カ月連続で増加した。ドイツの設備投
資意欲の回復が背景にあるとみられる1。米国（同＋5.6％）からの受注も 2 カ月連続で増加し
た。中国（同▲4.6％）からの受注は5 カ月ぶりに減少したが、依然高水準だ（図表7）。 
工作機械受注は 8 月分がすでに公表されており、内需は前月比＋6.6％と 3 カ月連続で増加
し、外需も同＋8.4％と3 カ月連続で増加した。いずれも増加基調を維持している。

【先行き】民需（船電除く）の緩やかな増加基調を見込むも中東情勢が下振れリスク 
先行きの民需（船電除く）は、緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。企業の設備投資意欲は
強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待されよう。 
内閣府・財務省「法人企業景気予測調査」 （2026 年 7-9 月期調査、調査時点は8 月 15 日）に
よると、大企業製造業では当期（9 月末）の設備判断 BSI（ 「不足」－「過大」 ）が上昇し、4 四
半期連続の「不足」超となった（図表8 左）。大企業非製造業では設備判断BSI は横ばい圏で推
移しており、引き続き「不足」超となっている（図表8 右）。 
日本政策投資銀行（DBJ）の「全国設備投資計画調査（2026 年6 月） 」では、2026 年度の大企
業（資本金 10 億円以上）・全産業（電力除く）の国内設備投資計画が前年度比＋15.4％の見通
しとなり2、前年同時期の調査結果（2025 年度の国内設備投資計画、同＋15.0％）を小幅に上
回った3。設備投資計画では実際の投資額よりも高めの数字が示される傾向があるものの、2025
年度の投資額の実績が同＋6.9％だったことを踏まえると、今年度も引き続き高い伸びとなる
ことが期待される。今年度計画の内訳を見ると、製造業（同＋13.2％）では、AI・データセン
ター関連投資を中心に増加する見込みだ。非製造業（電力除く、同＋16.8％）では、駅前再開
発や鉄道の新線建設、インバウンド関連投資などが全体をけん引するとみられている。 
ただし、中東情勢の悪化に伴い、受注が下振れするリスクには注意が必要だ。ホルムズ海峡
の通航が困難な状況が続く中、紅海側を通る代替ルートでパイプラインが攻撃を受けるなど4、
エネルギー供給を巡る先行き不透明感は強い。事業環境の不確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高
騰・供給不足に対する懸念から、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある。 
図表 8：機械受注額と設備投資 BSI（大企業）

（注 1）BSI は「不足」－「過大」社数構成比。直近2 期は今回調査での翌期、翌々期の見通し。

### Page 1

1. Photonics IC scaling up: AI drives data transmission speed and bandwidth 
requirement, leading to strong demand on Photonics IC, requesting it to catch up 
Electrical IC automated production and testing ecosystem in 1 to 2 years, while EIC / 
semiconductors has been developed for decades. The strong demand brings 
opportunities to tool suppliers, across coupling and testing, which are necessary 
tools to accelerate PIC production. Robotechnik / FiconTEC describes the numbers of 
tools they target to ship in coming 1 year would be over 50% of what they’ve shipped 
in past 25 years, reflecting the strong demand and orders visibility.  
2. Testing - CPO earliest beneficiary: As a new technology, CPO naturally takes time 
and faces challenges in the path of commercialization; however, we identify testing 
equipment suppliers as the earliest beneficiaries, considering the expensive final 
module means multiple testing during the production process is necessary, given the 
earlier a failure is found, the lower cost a failure is. As a result, companies we visited 
continue to see Insertion 2 (double sided wafer-level testing) as necessary, and 
Robotechnik / FiconTEC launched the new generation in 3Q26, which fully complied 
with the global leading foundry’s communication infrastructure. 
3. Testing - shortening time with KGD (know good die) at early stage: To facilitate 
photonics IC scaling up, not only are more tools needed but better tools, requiring 
suppliers’ strong R&D and execution, delivering continuous specification upgrade. 
Robotechnik / FiconTEC, as global leaders in PIC testing, aim to shorten the 
wafer-level testing time by 50-60% next year, and target 4x faster die-level testing 
next year. Enlitech (7728.TWO, Not Covered) offers NightJar, a hyperspectral image 
system, revealing AWG (arrayed waveguide grating) optical loss pathways with 
absolute optical power mapping, allowing customers to KGD at Insertion 1 (PIC wafer

### Page 2

testing) or Insertion 0 (PIC wafer early defect screening), saving failure costs. 
4. Coupling - automated production becoming necessary in high-speed connection: 
AI drives optical module towards high speed and high density / bandwidth, driving 
demand on automated production. GMT (4573.TWO, Not Covered) showcased dual FA 
active alignment equipment for 1.6T optical module, which could do the coupling for 
both Tx and Rx simultaneously, saving 50% of coupling time compared to traditional 
equipment. FitTech (6706.TW, Not Covered) showcased optical engine assembly system, 
featuring with passive / active alignment and customized development of coupling 
algorithms, which could be used in multiple products: high-speed optical modules, ELS, 
OCS, NPO, CPO, SiPh and optical chiplets. ASMPT showcased MEGA multi-chip bonding 
solution for complex multi-chip and photonics packaging applications, combining 
high-precision placement with adhesive dispensing, UV fixation and 3D inspection in one 
platform. 
5. FAU & Laser specification upgrade: With optical networking expanding from scale 
out to scale up, FAU and laser specification is also migrating. We estimate FOCI‘s FAU in 
2026E to be mainly 40 channels (starting in 3Q26) for 3.2T, followed by 80 channels 
(within 2026E) for 6.4T, and 100 channels under R&D. The company’s FAU enjoys several 
customers across fabless, foundry, laser suppliers, etc. FOCI Aug revenues accelerated to 
+20% MoM (vs. +9% MoM in Jul), reflecting the ramp up of high-end FAU. VPEC‘s 
optoelectronics revenues enjoy strong growth on the back of strong CW lasers demand 
and InP substrate supply getting better. Its InP epiwafer for CW lasers is also migrating 
from 70mW / 100mW (scale out) to above 100mW (scale up, NPO) and 300mW / 
400mW (scale up, CPO), along with expansion from CW lasers to 100G / 200G EML, 
supporting its growth ahead. 
6. AI drives CMP slurry: Anji Micro owns a comprehensive customer base, allowing it to 
ride on growing AI spending across China and overseas. The global-tier AI infrastructure 
continues to migrate, driving the CMP slurry consumption per system, along with AI 
server racks ramp up, enlarging the total addressable market. As the CMP slurry local 
leader, we are positive on Anji’s capability to offer qualified and reliable semi material 
products to penetrate global-tier clients.   
Company highlights

Robotechnik (300757.SZ, Buy, 12m TP at Rmb796) Chairman & Head of FiconTest 
visit: We hosted Robotechnik Chairman and Head of FiconTest and visited the booth in 
Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3. Management of Robotechnik highlights the strong demand on 
Photonics IC automated production and testing, driving their tools demand. The 
company’s strong beat in 2Q26 results (report link) reaffirms our positive view on 
Robotechnik, riding on the growing trend across CPO, NPO, OCS, and optical modules. 
We identify Robotechnik to be the major beneficiary in the photonics IC scaling trend, 
considering its comprehensive tools coverage and strong automation capability across 
software and hardware. Read more: Robotechnik / FiconTEC Semicon Taiwan 
takeaways.  
VPEC (2455.TW, Buy, 12m TP at NT$793), Chairman visit: We hosted VPEC Chairman 
and CFO in Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3. Management remains positive on their

optoelectronics business, riding on the AI infrastructure cycle, specification upgrades,
VPEC’s customers’ penetration, and product expansion. We remain positive on VPEC and
the company’s strong Jul and Aug revenues reaffirm the strong AI data center optical
networking demand and InP substrate supply improvement. Read more: VPEC Semicon
Taiwan takeaways.
Enlitech (7728.TWO, Not Covered), Chairman visit: We hosted Enlitech Chairman,
CEO, and CTO in Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3. Key discussions focused on CPO testing and

how the company’s product, NightJar, facilitating KGD (know good die) at early stage,
supporting customers to save failure costs. NightJar is a hyperspectral image system,
which could seamlessly upgrade the existing eVue microscope on PIC wafer-level testing
equipment (insertion 1), allowing customers to know the AWG (arrayed waveguide
grating) optical loss pathways with absolute optical power mapping. Read more:
Enlitech Semicon Taiwan takeaways.

FOCI (3363.TWO, Buy, 12m TP at NT$833), CFO visit: We hosted FOCI CFO in Semicon
Taiwan on Sep 3. Management remains positive on their CPO FAU business and expects
strong earnings growth ahead, riding on AI server racks ramp up and FAU specification
upgrade (from 40 channels to 100 channels). FOCI’s FAU started shipment in 3Q26,
driving Aug revenues growth accelerating to +20% MoM (vs. +9% MoM in July). We
expect CPO FAU revenue growth to further accelerate in 2H27, driving CPO FAU revenue

contribution up to 58% / 73% in 2027 / 28E (vs. 21% in 2026E), supporting GM
expansion to 37.9% by 2028E (vs. 18.8% in 2025A). Read more: FOCI Semicon Taiwan
takeaways.
GMT (4573.TWO, Not Covered): We visited GMT booth in Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3.
GMT showcased four launched system covering wafer-level optical inspection and
testing, as well as optical-engine active alignment for 1.6T optical modules. The
company highlighted automation and simultaneous TX/RX coupling, saving coupling

time by 50% compared to traditional methodology. Read more: GMT Semicon Taiwan
takeaways.
Fittech (6706.TW, Not Covered): We visited Fittech booth in Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3.
The company showcased equipment solutions for lens and FAU inspection,
high-precision optical coupling and bonding, PIC chip testing, etc. Management expects
FitTech to ride on the rising optical networking device end demand in AIDC and
specification upgrade towards 1.6T, 3.2T, CPO, NPO, driving the company’s growth

ahead. Read more: Fittech Semicon Taiwan takeaways.
Anji Micro (688019.SS, Buy, 12m TP at Rmb509): We visited Anji Micro booth in
Semicon Taiwan on Sep 3. The company demonstrated products incl. CMP Slurry, Post
CMP Cleaner etc. during the event. Key discussions focused on (1) CMP Slurry product
upgrade, (2) semi client demand, and (3) diversified customer base. Overall,
management is positive on the rising capex spending from both memory and advanced

logic clients, along with the specification upgrade towards high-end products. Read
more: Anji Micro Semicon Taiwan takeaways.
ASMPT (0522.HK, Neutral, 12m TP at HK$177): We visited ASMPT booth in Semicon
Taiwan on Sep 3. ASMPT showcased its advanced packaging solutions for AI, HPC and
silicon photonics and CPO applications, including TCB and HB technologies, alongside
the MEGA platform for high-precision multi-chip assembly. Read more: ASMPT Semicon

### Page 4

Taiwan takeaways.
Price target methodology and risks - ASMPT
Valuation methodology: We are Neutral-rated on ASMPT with a 12-month target price
of HK$177, which is based on 24.9x 2030E discounted P/E (discounted back to 2027E).
Our target P/E is derived from correlation between peers P/E to the sum of NI YoY and
OPM.
Key upside/downside risks: 1) customers’ faster-/slower-than-expected adoption of
Advanced Packaging tools; 2) stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand from

automotive customers; and 3) stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for traditional
IC packaging and SMT equipment.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Anji Micro
Valuation: We are Buy-rated on Anji Micro with a 12-month target price of Rmb509,
which is based on 46x 2030E discounted P/E (discounted back to 2027E). Our target P/E
is derived from correlation between peers P/E to the sum of NI YoY and OPM.
Key downside risks: 1) supply chain risks; 2) weaker semi client demand; and 3)

weaker-than-expected product expansion.
Valuation: Our 12-month target price of NT$833 is based on a 15.4x 2030E P/E,
discounted back to 2027E at a COE of 12.6%. Our target multiple is derived from peers’
correlation between forward-year trading P/E and earnings growth.
Key downside risks: Fiercer-than-expected market competition, technology migration
trends in optical networking, and slower-than-expected capacity ramp-up.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - VPEC

Valuation: We adopt a discounted P/E method and apply a 22.2x target P/E on VPEC’s
2029E EPS, discounting back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%
and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12m TP of NT$793. Our target P/E is
based on a 0.4x PEG&M ratio derived from the average level of peers in the photonics
supply chain.
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh business ramp up; weaker-than-expected
microelectronics demand from smartphones clients; IDM offers EPI wafer in-house.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - RoboTechnik
Valuation: Our 12-month target price of Rmb796 is based on a 20.7x 2031E P/E,
discounted back to 2027E at a COE of 11.3%. Our target multiple is derived from the
correlation between consumer electronic peers’ PEG&M (2026E P/E to 2027E NI YoY and
OPM), with RoboTechnik’s 2032E net income at 2% YoY and 2032E OPM at 32%.
Key downside risks: Fiercer-than-expected market competition; lower-than-expected

optical connection end demand; worse-than-expected photovoltaic market downcycle.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Greater China Semiconductors  | Asia Pacific
Quantum Security / eMemory:
Investor Feedback
Low investor expectations, mostly a teach-in – eMemory
remains under-owned and under-researched, so feedback was
largely educational rather than push-back; we see this as the
marketing opportunity.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What we published – and the feedback. On 17 August we released the Global
Foundation “Hardware Quantum Security – Building Defenses for Q-Day and
Beyond” with our eMemory OW initiation (PT NT$3,330): as quantum computing
nears (base case ~2030), a new hardware-security layer must be deployed 3-5 years
ahead, with physical unclonable function (PUF)-based Root-of-Trust IP adding 5-10%

to the semi IP market. Feedback was constructive – clients agree the “Harvest Now,
Decrypt Later” risk and 2030-31 migration deadlines make this a multi-year, non-
binary theme. The recurring point –  and our marketing opportunity – is that  Aspeed
is already well-owned while eMemory stays under-researched by global funds.
Debate 1 – sizing the TAM. The key question is whether PUF is truly required once
post-quantum cryptography (PQC) arrives. We size a PUF-PQC TAM of ~US$310mn

by 2030 (base), US$600mn bull / US$190mn bear, on ~25% PQC adoption and
~50% PUF attach, with datacenter/AI servers the largest royalty pool. Once PQC is
implemented, secure key generation/storage makes PUF the logical anchor – so
sensitivity sits on PQC pace, not PUF attach. We view eMemory’s NeoPUF as best-in-
class: near-zero bit-error rate, lower area/power, and superior advanced-node scaling
vs. SRAM PUF.
Debate 2 – is eMemory the right proxy? Yes. The case rests on the core royalty

engine: royalties are the majority of earnings and tied to customer production, so as
mix shifts to leading-edge nodes, higher wafer values lift royalty per design-win.
Advanced-node projects are already >50% of licensing  non-recurring engineering
(NRE) revenue. This is corroborated by the just-reported August sales of NT
$357mn, +42.8% Y/Y (8M26 +21.9% Y/Y, 8th straight month of growth);
royalties were 60.8% of 2Q26 revenue (+7.8% Y/Y) and management guides for

royalty growth to accelerate into 2H26 on advanced-node ASPs and new apps (OTP,
NeoFlash/1T Flash, PUF-PQC). (Continued in the next section)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Under-owned, mostly a teach-in: feedback was largely educational, not push-back
– the marketing opportunity; clients called the level “a good entry point”.
August sales momentum: NT$357mn, +42.8% Y/Y; 8M26 +21.9% Y/Y (8th
straight month of Y/Y growth).
Reiterate Overweight, PT NT$3,330: ~30% further upside, plus free optionality

on a US$310mn base-case PUF-PQC TAM by 2030.
September 8, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

2

Our view and stock calls. Reiterate OW, PT NT$3,330 (~30% upside from NT$2,570) – a 
rare mix of a defensive, high-visibility royalty stream (100% gross margin, ~63% ROE) plus 
free optionality on the post-quantum build-out, alongside Synopsys (OW) and adopter 
Aspeed (OW). Catalysts: monthly-sales momentum, the advanced-node royalty ramp, PQC 
design-win news-flow and San Jose OCP in Oct 2026. Risks: 1) semi downturn, 2) PUF-PQC 
competition, 3) slower quantum/PQC adoption. Net-net, eMemory is an under-owned 
name where the royalty re-rating and quantum-security theme are both intact.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
eMemory Technology Inc (3529.TWO)
We derive our price target from a residual income model. We assume an 8.6% cost of equity
(2.0% risk-free rate, 6.0% equity risk premium, and 1.1 beta), a payout ratio of 90%, a medium-
term earnings growth rate of 23.0%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.4%. Based on these
assumptions, our RI model implies an equity value of NT$249bn, or NT$3,330 per share.

Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected PUF adoption
n Stronger-than-expected advanced-node OTP migration
n Higher-than-expected royalty expansion from wafer value growth
Risks to Downside
n Slower-than-expected adoption by key customers
n Slower-than-expected PQC adoption
n Increasing geopolitical and trade policy risks
Aspeed Technology (5274.TWO)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions:
n Cost of equity of 9.8% (2.0% risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.3 beta)

n Medium-term growth rate of 21.4%
n Terminal growth rate of 5.5%
n Cash payout ratio of 85%
Risks to Upside
n Stronger cloud demand
n Faster-than-expected spec migration
n Mild competition
Risks to Downside
n Softening cloud demand
n Slower-than-expected spec migration
n Intensified competition
n Further policy tightening in China
Synopsys Inc. (SNPS.O)
The sector is trading on a 30-35x P/E multiple range. We expect Synopsys to trade in line and

apply a c.33x P/E multiple to estimated FY26 EPS ($15.12).
Risks to Upside
n Bigger ramp-up in AI ASICs and GPUs than modelled
n Quicker recovery in Automotive & industrial
n Design wins at expense of peers with Ansys products

Risks to Downside
n Scale and duration of downturn seeing deeper R&D cuts
n Further tightening of export controls
n Loss of share to new entrant EDA players given localisation trends
n Failure to hit cost synergies from Ansys merger

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

ab 15 September 2026
Global Research
First Read
Voltronic Power
UBS Taiwan Summit 2026 key takeaways:
Earnings growth hinges on UPS/HVDC deliveries
Sales turning to growth in H226; sales mix change driving margin expansion
Management indicated bookings and shipments remained solid in July-Aug despite
temporary PCB shortages, and it expects a sales turnaround to growth in H226. Inverter
sales could stay weak on competition and Voltronic's product portfolio adjustments

(reducing low-margin products). Meanwhile, increasing outsourcing by brands will
support UPS sales growth. Management expects HVDC products to start contributing in
2027. Voltronic added a third customer for HVDC products. We think a higher sales
portion from UPS/HVDC products and inverter product adjustments could bring GM
expansion in 2027.
UPS growth intact;  HVDC and PDU represent the next legs of growth
The company expects sales to resume growth by single digits  in H226  and expects >10%

YoY growth in 2027. Management remains cautious about solar  inverters due to intense
competition from Chinese peers and expects inverter sales to decline as Voltronic adjusts
its product portfolio, focusing more on commercial solar  and premium products. For
2027, management expects offline UPS to grow 0-5%, supported by outsourcing
opportunities and emerging market demand, which could offset notebook weakness.
High-power UPS could account for an increasingly higher sales portion, driven by

accelerating outsourcing by tier 1 customers. For HVDC, management highlighted it
obtained one more client. HVDC product progress is on schedule, with 800VDC power
shelf sampling expected by year-end, a pilot run in H127 and mass production in H227.
The company is preparing testing and production equipment at factories in Taiwan and
China. Over the longer term, management also sees opportunities in PDUs, a market it
estimates at US$5bn in 2025, with a 12-15% sales CAGR over the next 5-8 years.

Margin  improvement on product mix optimisation and higher prices
Voltronic has implemented two UPS price increases this year to offset higher raw
material costs, which it expects to be reflected on its P&L starting in Sept 2026. For solar
inverters, management plans to focus on higher power and premium products with a
GM above 20%, while reducing low-power products with a GM at or below 20% over
the next two to three quarters, which could support GM expansion. In upcoming

quarters, solar inverter products may shrink to <20% of the total sales mix, while higher-
margin businesses such as high-power UPS and HVDC products continue to ramp. We
think the sales mix change will expand margins in 2027.
Valuation: Maintain Buy rating and PT of NT$1,128
We maintain our Buy rating and price target of NT$1,128, based on sum-of-the-parts
analysis, applying 28x/23x/24x 2027E PE for UPS/inverter/PSU business, respectively.

Company Description
Founded in 2008, Voltronic is a leading ODM for uninterruptible power supply (UPS) and
inverters. Its major customers include Schneider, Vertiv, and Riello. Voltronic is headquartered
in Taiwan and has production plants in mainland China, Vietnam, and Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement

We base our price target on sum of the parts.
We believe the key risks for Voltronic include: 1) a slower-than-expected outsourcing trend
from leading branded UPS companies; 2) a faster-than-expected ramp-up of the electricity
grid in developing countries resulting in lower-than-expected demand for off-grid solar
systems; 3) FX risks; and 4) rising raw material prices.
Key industry-specific risks include: 1) unexpected changes in the underlying global

macroeconomic environment; 2) currency and interest rate movement; and 3) commodity
price inflation.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

營收走勢圖

研究員/蔡佾璋 EASONTSAI@uni-psg.com

研究部維持中傑 -KY(6965)投資評等為中立，主要考量： (1)中傑雖
2Q26 營運轉盈，單季稅後純益4,651 萬元，QoQ 虧轉盈，-77.5%YoY，
稅後 EPS 0.3 元，仍低於研究部預估的0.74 元，1H26 仍虧損 5417 萬
元，稅後EPS-0.34 元。 (2)目前全球大環境消費景氣持續疲弱、不確
定性高，歐美客戶下單態度謹慎保守，且 2H26 仍處策略調整階段，產
能利用率預期較 2Q26 下降，因此公司表示對 2026 及 2027 年營收保
守看待。(3)雖公司現已著手提升高毛利訂單比重，但仍在調整期，效
益尚未充分顯現，短期毛利率改善幅度有限。(4)印尼新廠於 2027 年
初開始投產，初期有產能爬坡及學習曲線，折舊固定成本比重較高 ，
因此對毛利率仍保守看待。研究部認為公司短期要回到營運狀較佳的
15%以上毛利率之機率較低，預估中傑-KY 2026 年、2027 年稅後 EPS 
2.06 元(原估 3.19 元)、4.83 元，分別為-40%YoY、+134.4%，過往評
價區間為 10-25 倍，目前本益比約 14 倍，評價合理，故維持中立評
等，目標價 72 元(15xPER *2027EPS)。

重點摘要： 
1. 中傑-KY為運動鞋、休閒鞋和皮鞋製造大廠

圖 1、中傑-KY 2Q26 產品營收佔比

資料來源：中傑-KY、統一投顧整理

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

中傑-KY成立於1983年，發跡於台中大雅，以皮鞋起家，目前
主要業務是鞋類產品生產 、 代工 ， 以運動鞋 、 休閒鞋和皮鞋為
大宗。台灣為營運與接單總部，中國、越南與印尼廠區均為
100%全資持股，印度廠則採51%持股之合資模式營運。公司的
8個生產基地主要分佈在中國、越南、印度，2025年總產量為
3,233萬雙 ，-20.2%YoY，2025年總產能約為4,122萬雙/年，推
算稼動率約78%，目前員工人數約為 2.8萬人(6月底為3.2萬
人)。 出貨雙數方面，2Q26出貨量810.3萬雙，+3.1%YoY， 相較
1Q26的-19.7%YoY有明顯好轉， 1H26累積出貨量為 1524.4萬
雙，-9%YoY，仍呈現衰退。

營收方面 ，前三大運動品牌客戶2Q26皆有所成長 ， 然1H26仍均
呈現衰退。客戶營收占比方面，1H26年前三大品牌占比68%，
維持在7成左右 ， 其中 ， 最大客戶為New Balance，由2025年占
比51%降至1H26的47%； 第二大客戶為安踏ANTA， 占比維持11%；
第三大客戶李寧1H26營收佔比從9%提升至10%， 其他主要代工
客戶還有營收占比約3%的UA， 以及Columbia、SOREL、Clarks、
ARIAT等國際知名品牌。

圖 4、中傑-KY 1H26 品牌客戶營收佔比

資料來源：中傑-KY、統一投顧整理 
2. 預估3Q26稅後EPS 1.4元，+293%YoY 
2Q26營收56.6億元，+18.3%QoQ，+8.4%YoY，營收成長主要因
出貨雙數提升。公司著手提升高毛利訂單比重，因此毛利率
12.7%(原估10.6%)，+2.7pctQoQ，+1.1pctYoY，優於預期， 然
費用率與稅率也同步提高，故 稅後純益4,651萬元，QoQ虧轉
盈，-77.5%YoY，稅後EPS 0.3元，低於研究部預估的0.74元。

展望3Q26，中傑7+8月合計營收37.6億元，+2.9%YoY，營收達
成率68.3%，符合預期，因消費景氣持續疲弱，品牌客戶下單
謹慎且以急單為主 ， 訂單能見度有限 ， 對2H26訂單與營收展望
保守看待。故研究部預估3Q26營收55億元(原估61.8億元)，-
2.9%QoQ，+2%YoY， 毛利率僅較2Q26略增至13.6%(原估13.3%)，
營業利益2.8億元，+81.8%QoQ，+614.3%YoY，稅後EPS 1.4元
(原估1.61元)，+372%QoQ，+293%YoY。

3. 預估2026、2027稅後EPS 2.06元、4.83元，分別為-40%YoY、
+134.4%YoY 
展望2026年 ， 目前大環境不確定性高 ， 歐美客戶相對保守 ， 因
此研究部預估中傑2026年營收212.4億元(原估227.3億元)，-
2%YoY，受原物料價格上漲影響，毛利率面臨較大壓力，僅回
升至12.6%(原估12%)，+1pctYoY，營業利益5.5億元(原估5.3
億元)，+21.2%YoY， 稅後EPS 2.06元(原估3.19元)，-40%YoY。

研究部預估中傑2027年營收211.5億元，-0.4%YoY，印尼新廠
於2027年初開始投產 ， 初期有產能爬坡及學習曲線 ， 折舊固定
成本比重較高，預期毛利率僅將回升至13.8%，+1.2pctYoY，
營業利益8.9億元 ，+63.4%YoY， 稅後EPS 4.83元，+134.4%YoY。

© 2010 統 一 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 。 本 公 司 提 供 之 報 告 內 容 係 根 據 本 公
司 認 可 之 資 料來 源 ， 並 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 但 不 保 證 其 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 ， 報
告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之

### Page 1

ACCI–Westpac
Survey of Industrial Trends
Australian Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking Corporation
260th report September 2026 (survey conducted from 20 July to 3 September 2026)
 Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained sluggish through Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI
Actual Composite edging slightly lower from 50.5 in Q2 to 49.1 in Q3, consistent with a neutral-to-slightly-
contractionary reading.
 Manufacturers are contending with numerous headwinds at present. As the Middle East conflict has continued

to unfold, the combination of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering uncertainty has made for a
more challenging environment for consumer demand and some parts of the construction sector.
 Growth in new orders remained absent in Q3, with a net 2% of respondents reporting a decrease after only a
net 1% reported an increase in Q2. Sluggish demand saw a similar net percentage of manufacturers wind back
production, reduce headcount and decrease overtime. Manufacturers instead chipped away at existing order

backlogs and built up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
 Spillovers from the Middle East conflict continue to play a significant role when it comes to cost pressures.
A net 48% of respondents reported an increase in average unit costs during Q3, barely changed from the net
51% reporting an increase in Q2. Pressures remain particularly acute in sub-sectors with more direct exposure
to fuel, fertiliser and other supply chain stresses, including machinery and equipment, fabricated and primary

metals, chemicals, wood and furniture.
 Despite persistent cost pressures, the proportion of firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell to a
net 24% in Q3. This remained well below the proportion reporting higher average unit costs, suggesting
manufacturers continue to absorb part of the increase in costs rather than fully passing them on to customers,
placing pressure on margins.
 The Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved confidence

in future activity. Stronger expectations for new orders and output suggest manufacturers expect the current
period of soft demand to be relatively short lived.
 However, while manufacturers may see some light at the end of the tunnel for demand and production, they
do not see a significant let-up in cost pressures anytime soon, with a net 43% anticipating a further increase
in average unit costs over the next three months. Ongoing margin squeeze is the baseline view among

manufacturers, contributing to a weaker outlook for profit growth.
 Manufacturers remain somewhat cautious about their investment plans for the year ahead. On balance, a net
14% of firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over the next twelve months, while a net 4%
intend to increase building investment. While expectations have turned more bullish in some areas, until such
outcomes materialise, some level of hesitancy may persist.

The Survey of Industrial Trends produced by the Australian
Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking
Corporation is a quarterly publication.
It is Australia's longest running business survey dating
from 1966, providing a timely update on manufacturing and

insights into economy-wide trends.

Lonergan Research
Net response or "balance" is calculated by the proportion of
"ups" less "downs" on individual questions, thereby yielding
the net balance. A positive balance indicates a net upward
or improving trend and a minus balance a net downward or
deteriorating trend.

The 260th consecutive survey was closed on Monday, x
September 2026 A total of 413 responses were received,
and provided a reasonable cross–section of Australian
manufacturing in respect of industry groups and size of
operation.

Key Survey Results
September 2026
• Manufacturing sentiment about the general business
outlook deteriorated sharply last quarter, as manufacturers
were confronted with acute cost pressures and a stalling in
new orders growth.
• Sentiment remains pessimistic but conditions improved

in Q3, with a net 12% of respondents expect the general
business situation to deteriorate over the next six months,
compared to a net 21% anticipating a deterioration in Q2.
• While manufacturers are expecting an improvement in
demand and production near-term, the backdrop of rising
costs, higher interest rates and difficulties in sourcing
labour will continue to present challenges for businesses.
• Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained

sluggish in Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite
edging slightly lower from 50.5 to 49.1, consistent with a
neutral-to-slightly-contractionary reading.
• Underpinning the result were small declines across all of
the constituent components: output, new orders, overtime,
numbers employed and order backlogs.
• In contrast, the Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite
rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved
confidence in future activity. However, recent history has

shown that expectations can run ahead of actual future
outcomes for a period of time, and that could remain the
case should current headwinds persist.
• The Labour Market Composite held steady moving into the
second half of the year, nudging only slightly lower from
44.7 in Q2 to 44.5 in Q3.
• Results in the Labour Market Composite have foreshadowed
the trends in official labour market data for the market
sector, where jobs growth started to recover last year and

kick into a higher gear earlier this year.
• More recently, the Labour Market Composite has pointed to
a renewed slowing in jobs growth momentum. This would
be consistent with a moderation in labour demand over the
coming quarters as the effects of slower economic growth
from high inflation and interest rates continue to weigh on
the activity.

4
Manufacturing & the Business Cycle
• The Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite has a solid track
record as a timely indicator of near-term domestic economic
conditions and turning points in the cycle.
• The Q2 National Accounts showed that growth is slowing
but not as rapidly as initially feared. Underneath that is a

complex mix of headwinds and tailwinds. There are clear
drags coming from higher interest rates, elevated inflation,
slowing public investment and the housing market (albeit
so far confined to the direct effects from lower housing
turnover). However, these are being offset by tailwinds
related to firmer business investment, particularly in data
centres, energy and related infrastructure.
• This survey suggests softer demand and lingering cost
pressures remain a persistent themes that will continue to

shape momentum in local manufacturing and the domestic
economy over the coming months.
The Business Cycle & Economic Outlook
Manufacturing & Business Investment
• The ACCI-Westpac survey has historically provided a broad
gauge on equipment spending trends in the manufacturing
sector. However, actual spending has persistently under
performed the investment intentions in this survey,
suggesting that some plans are failing to materialise.
• Official data had been turning more constructive, but

momentum looks to have slipped once again. The National
Accounts showed that an investment pulse is underway in
Australia, but it is being chiefly driven by a few sectors –
particularly data centres and renewables. Outside of these
sectors, growth in equipment spending is more subdued.
• Ongoing uncertainty surrounding the Middle East conflict,
together with elevated cost pressures, higher interest
rates and a subdued demand outlook are likely to remain a

headwind for investment.
Australian & World Manufacturing Surveys
• Global and Australian manufacturing cycles tend to be
synchronised, especially at major turning points. The
Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite typically moves in line
with global manufacturing conditions, albeit with some
level of variation within any given cycle.
• However, more recently, trends have started to diverge
across the major manufacturing hubs. The US continues to
weather the headwinds associated with tariff uncertainty

and the US-Iran war, with the ISM PMI at 54.6 in August,
firmly in expansionary territory. It is a similar story in
Europe, where the S&P Global PMI has risen to 52.7 in
August, its highest in many years. Meanwhile, China's
manufacturing remains relatively lacklustre, similar to
Australia, with the NBS PMI around breakeven territory at
49.8 as of August.

Activity & Orders
• Growth in new orders remained sluggish in Q3, with a net
2% of respondents reporting a decrease, easing from a net
1% reporting an increase in Q2.
• As the Middle East conflict continues, the combination
of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering

uncertainty has made for a more challenging environment
for consumer demand and some parts of the construction
sector.
• Manufacturers expect growth in new orders to improve
over the next three months, with a net 20% anticipating an
increase. Whether these expectations will be met given the
challenging backdrop is a key question.

Exports
Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Actual – net balance 9 8

• Growth in manufacturing exports were broadly stable
moving into the second half of the year, with a net 8% of
firms reporting an increase in Q3, following Q2's net 9%.
• Demand from our Asian trading partners have continued to
support processed food and agriculture exports. However,
a firmer AUD and uncertainty from the Middle East conflict

would have dampened growth in the quarter. US tariff risks
meanwhile remain a wildcard, although fortunately, it has
had little overall impact to date.
• Expectations have cooled compared to earlier in the year
but they remain positive, with a net 12% expecting an
increase in exports over the next three months.
• After slowing materially last quarter, output took another
step lower in Q3. A net 2% of firms reported a decrease in
output, the first decline in a year.

• Demand remained sluggish during the quarter, leading
manufacturers to chip away at existing order backlogs and
build up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
• Looking ahead, a net 26% of firms expect an increase in
output over the next three months, suggesting the current
soft patch is expected to only be temporary. However, it is
important to note that expectations have been more bullish
than actual future outcomes over the past two years.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
• Manufacturers turned more cautious about their investment
plans for the year ahead, following the onset of the Middle
East conflict.
• That broadly remained the case in Q3, though investment
intentions were still positive. On balance, a net 14% of
firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over

the next twelve months, while a net 4% intend to increase
building investment.
• There remains a significant degree of uncertainty around
the economic outlook. Expectations for production and
orders have turned more bullish, but until such outcomes
materialise, some level of hesitancy might persist.
Investment & Profitability
• Profit expectations took a substantial hit last quarter,
reflecting a combination of surging cost pressures, higher
interest rates and the onset of the Middle East conflict.

This saw a sharp increase in cost pressures and substantial
uncertainty around the outlook.
• With these headwinds persisting, the outlook for profits
was little changed. Only a net 10% of firms expect an
improvement in profits over the coming year, below the
historic average of a net 19%.
• While manufacturers are anticipating an improvement in
new orders over the coming months, cost pressures are
also expected to remain acute, compressing margins.

• Capacity utilisation has quickly swung from a period of
overutilisation earlier this year, prior to the onset of the
Middle East conflict, back toward underutilisation.
• In the latest survey, fewer firms reported operating at
above average capacity (23% to 18%) with more firms
reporting that they were operating at below average
capacity (25% to 28%). This saw an increase in the net
balance of firms reporting capacity underutilisation, up from
2% in Q2 to 10% in Q3.

• This is still a fairly robust result. Over the full history
of the survey, manufacturers have tended to report a
larger degree of underutilisation, especially during cycles
characterised by slower growth and/or higher interest
rates.

• Employment dynamics in the manufacturing sector remain
sluggish. A net 2% of firms reported a decline in the size
of the workforce in Q3, following on from a net 4% also
reporting a pull-back in Q2.
• Manufacturers were confronted with a stagnation in new
orders growth and pull-back in production during the

quarter, providing little scope to increase headcount.
• Over the next three months however, a net 7% of
firms expect to increase headcount. While modest, the
expected lift in employment coincides with manufacturers'
expectations for a rebound in new orders and production
over the coming months.
The Labour Market
• Historically, firms' views on the difficulty of finding labour
tracks shifts in the Australian unemployment rate, albeit
with some variation between cycles depending on the

industry mix of employment growth.
• In Q3, a larger share of manufacturers reported difficulty
in sourcing labour, with a net 28.3% indicating labour was
"harder to find" compared to three months ago.
• Overall jobs growth has held up well so far this year, but
the spill overs from the Middle East conflict and recent
interest rate rises are expected to weigh on jobs growth
over the coming months.
• Manufacturers' use of overtime deteriorated further in Q3,

with a net 3% reporting a decline, in line with expectations
from the prior quarter.
• Hours worked usually adjusts more quickly to shifts in
demand conditions than employment. The fact that both
eased materially at the same time points to a more abrupt
cooling in labour demand within the sector.
• Manufacturers anticipate a modest improvement in
overtime usage over the coming three months, with a net
5% expecting an increase. If manufacturers' expectations

for demand crystallise, some increase in overtime would be
required to avoid building up order backlogs.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
• Despite persistent unit cost pressures, the proportion of
firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell slightly
during Q3, to a net 24%.
• This was well below the net 48% reporting an increase in
average unit costs over the same period. It also fell well
short of manufacturers' own expectations from last quarter,

implying margins are being squeezed as firms only partially
pass through higher costs onto customers.
• Manufacturers expect only partial pass-through of higher
costs to customers will remain the case over the next
few months, with a net 27% expecting to increase selling
prices.
Prices & Inflation
• Results around wage expectations in the manufacturing
sector were broadly unchanged versus last quarter, with a
net 31% indicating they expect their next enterprise wage

agreement to delivery an outcome above the last.
• The survey suggests no real step-change in expectations
following the Fair Work Commission's 2026 Annual Wage
Review, which announced a larger-than-expected 4.75%
increase in award wages in Q2, to be implemented during
Q3. Expectations are still elevated, and this may compound
existing non-labour cost pressures for many manufacturers.
• Consistent with this survey, official ABS data points to a
turnaround in the wages growth trend, up to 3.3%yr in Q2,

slightly outstripping the national average of 3.2%yr.
• Manufacturers are grappling with acute cost pressures as
the supply shock stemming from the Middle East continues
to impact. In Q3, a net 48% of firms reported a rise in
average unit costs.
• Sub-sectors with more direct exposure to fuel, energy
intensive goods such as fertilisers, plastics and metals are
reporting more acute pressure, including machinery and
equipment, fabricated and primary metals, chemicals, wood

and furniture.
• Manufacturers do not see a significant let-up in cost
pressures near-term, with a net 43% anticipating a further
increase in average unit costs over the next three months.

Factors Limiting Production
Single Factor Most Limiting Production
Q1 2026 Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Orders (%) 29 35 36
Capacity (%) 11 10 8
Labour (%) 24 17 20
Finance (%)   12 10 12
Materials (%) 12 14 8
Other (%) 4 5 5
None (%) 8 9 11
• Orders remain an important constraint on production – a

lack of demand saw 36% of respondents cite this as the
single factor most limiting production. However, this is still
well below historical averages, showcasing the relative
importance of other supply-side limitations currently.
• Labour remains a clear example, with 20% citing it as the
primary constraint in Q3, above Q2's 17% and well above
the pre-pandemic average. Difficulties in sourcing labour
remains a persistent theme.
• Material shortages appear to have eased, with the

proportion of respondents citing it as the most limiting
factor down from 14% to 8%. While off its recent highs, it
is still well above the pre-pandemic average.
• Capacity dipped slightly to 8% in Q3, but the proportion
of respondents citing None has steadily risen over recent
quarters, up to 11% in Q3, suggesting that despite recent
disruptions and volatility, a decent portion of manufacturers'
production is returning back to some kind of 'norm'.

The Westpac–ACCI Composite Indices
The Westpac–ACCI Actual and Expected Composite indices are weighted averages of the activity measures in the survey. The 
weights are as follows: employment 20%; new orders 30%; output 25%; order backlog 15%; overtime 10% .
The Westpac–ACCI Labour Market Composite is a function of actual employment, with a weight of almost 50%, as well as: 
expected employment; expected overtime; new orders; order backlog; and expected order backlog.

Document 179: AIブームで、なぜ今「社債」なのか？ ～巨額のAI投資が変える金利環境と、個人マネーの行方～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIブームで、なぜ今「社債」なのか？ ～巨額のAI投資が変える金利環境と、個人マネーの行方～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 9 月 15 日

1/6 
AI ブームで、なぜ今「社債」なのか？  
～巨額の AI 投資が変える金利環境と、個人マネーの行方～ 
 政策調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 最近、国内の個人投資家の間で高利回りの社債が人気を集めている。こうした社債人気が高
まる一方、世界の社債市場では、「金利ある世界」への移行に加え、世界的な「AIブーム」
に伴う資金調達の構造変化が進んでいる。多くの投資家が AI 関連「株式」に熱狂する一方
で、巨大な資本市場である「社債市場」においても大きな構造変化が起きている。 
○ この社債市場の変化は、AI ブームと金利上昇が同時進行するなかで生じている3つの側面に
現れている。第一に、データセンターや半導体などへの巨額投資を背景に、ハイパースケー
ラー（注 1）による社債発行が急増している。第二に、金利上昇と借り換え需要が重なり、グ
ローバル市場では、高いクーポンを持つ社債の比重が高まっている。第三に、社債市場全体
において、投資信託やETFを通じた投資が拡大している。 
○ ETF の普及により、個人投資家もグローバルな高利回り社債市場へ容易に参加できるように
なった。株式などによるキャピタルゲインに関心が集まりやすい個人の資産形成において、
高い利息収入が期待できる社債ファンドは「インカムゲイン投資」を復活させる有力な選択
肢となる。 
○ しかし、「利回りが高いから買う」という単純な行動には注意が必要である。高い利回りの
裏には、AI投資競争における企業の「信用リスク」や、市場ストレス時にファンドからの資
金流出が価格下落を増幅させる「流動性リスク」が潜んでいる。個人投資家には、これらの
リスクを理解した上で、 自らのポートフォリオ全体の中でインカムゲインの役割を再設計し、
管理していく姿勢が求められる。

1. AIブームと「金利ある世界」が交差する市場 
最近、日本の個人投資家の間で、ソフトバンクグループなどが発行する「高利回りの社債」が
強い人気を集めている。好条件の銘柄は販売開始直後に完売となるケースも珍しくない。長らく
続いた低金利時代が終わり、 「金利ある世界」が戻ってきたことで、インカムゲインを狙う投資家
の資金が社債市場に向かっているのである。 
こうした国内の社債人気が高まる一方、世界の社債市場では、AIブームを背景とする新たな資

### Page 2

2/6 
金調達の動きが広がっている。多くの個人投資家は、AIの成長果実を求めてNVIDIAなどのAI関連
企業の「株式」への投資に熱を上げてきた。しかし、AI技術の実装には、データセンターや半導
体などへの莫大なインフラ投資が欠かせない。世界の主要なハイパースケーラーでは、巨額の投
資資金を内部資金だけでなく、社債によって調達する動きが急速に広がっている。 
本稿では、 「金利ある世界」への移行と「AIの巨額投資」が重なる今、世界の社債市場にどのよ
うな構造変化が起きているのかをデータから解き明かしたい。そして、国内だけでなく世界に目
を向け、従来よりも高いクーポンの社債が増える動きが、個人の資産形成において、利息収入を
得る「インカムゲイン投資」を復活させる契機となるのかを検討する。

2. AIブームと「金利ある世界」がもたらす社債市場の 3 つの変化 
(1) AIの巨額投資が社債発行を押し上げる 
第一の変化は、ハイパースケーラーによる社債市場を通じた資金調達が急速に拡大しているこ
とである。AI関連事業は、従来型のソフトウェア事業とは異なり、データセンターの建設や半導
体の確保、さらには電力インフラの整備に、継続的かつ巨額の先行投資を必要とする。ハイパー
スケーラーは、AI関連事業の拡大を通じて、設備集約型企業としての性格を強めている。こうし
た巨額の資金需要を内部資金だけで賄うことは難しくなりつつあり、社債市場を通じた資金調達
が急速に拡大している。 
Bank of Englandの「Financial Stability Report（2026年 7月） 」によると、ハイパースケー
ラーによる投資適格社債の発行は急増している。資料1が示す通り、2026年は上期だけで既に
2025 年通年の発行額を突破した。これら主要ハイパースケーラー5社の社債残高は、2025年末時
点では米国の投資適格社債残高全体の3％に過ぎなかったが、2026年初から5月初旬までの新規
発行額においては15％超を占めるに至っている。AIインフラ構築に向けた巨額の資金需要が、社
債市場の供給を力強く押し上げているのである。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI ハイパースケーラーの社債発行額の推移（通貨別） 
（注）単位は 10 億米ドル。対象は主要 AI ハイパースケーラー5 社。2026 年は上期までの実績。 
（出所）Bank of England, “Financial Stability Report, July 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 高いクーポンを持つ社債の比重が上昇 
第二の変化は、発行済み社債のクーポンの構成が、より高い水準へシフトしていることであ
る。AIブームに伴う資金需要の拡大は、歴史的な低金利時代が終わりを迎えた「金利ある世界」
において進行している。企業は、新規の資金調達だけでなく、過去に低金利で発行した既存債務
の借り換え（リファイナンス）においても、より高い金利での調達を余儀なくされている。その
結果、発行済み社債の中で、より高いクーポンを持つ社債の割合が高まっている。 
OECDの「Global Debt Report 2026」によれば、発行済み社債のクーポンは明確に上昇傾向にあ
る。資料2 を見ると、世界の投資適格社債では、残高ベースでクーポン4％超の社債が50％を占
めている一方で、クーポン2％以下の社債が残高に占める割合は、2021年には約4分の1を占め
ていたが、足元では14％まで低下した。さらに、よりリスクの高い非投資適格債（ハイイールド
債）市場においては、クーポン8％以上の社債が残高ベースで15％を占めており、2022年の 9％
から顕著に上昇している。より高いクーポンを持つ社債の比重は、投資適格社債、非投資適格社
債の双方で高まっている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 世界の社債残高の推移（クーポン別） 
（注）非金融企業の社債残高ベース。GFC は世界金融危機（Global Financial Crisis）を示す。 
（出所）OECD, “Global Debt Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 高利回り社債の買い手としてファンド・ETFの存在感が高まる 
第三の変化は、高利回り社債の買い手としての投資信託やETF（上場投資信託）の台頭である。
これらのファンドによる社債保有が拡大しており、一般の個人投資家もファンドを通じて高利回
り社債市場へ容易に参加できるようになっている。 
IMFの「Global Financial Stability Report（2025年10月） 」は、ハイイールド債市場におけ
る投資家層の変化を指摘している。資料3 によれば、米国のハイイールド債市場において、投資
信託（オープンエンド型ファンド）およびETFが保有する割合は、2015年の 37％から2024年には
45％へと大きく上昇した。これは個人投資家にとって高利回り社債市場へのアクセスを広げる一
方で、市場環境が悪化した際には、解約売りを通じた価格変動リスク（ボラティリティ）を増幅
させる要因にもなり得る点には留意が必要である。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 米国債券市場における投資信託・ETF の保有シェア 
（注）単位は％。各市場における投資信託（オープンエンド型ファンド）および ETF の保有割合を示す。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2025”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 個人投資家に求められる「インカムゲインの再設計」 
AI 企業の旺盛な資金需要と金利上昇を背景に、社債市場はインカムゲインを得られる投資先と
して見直されつつある。これまで機関投資家が中心であった高利回り社債（ハイイールド債な
ど）も、投資信託やETFの普及により、個人投資家にとって極めて身近な選択肢となっている。
株式の価格変動を警戒しつつも、株式とは異なる形でAI関連企業の成長に投資し、利息収入を得
たい投資家にとって、社債ファンドは有力な選択肢となるだろう。 
もっとも、投資しやすくなったからといって、判断が容易になったわけではない。 「利回りが高
いから買う」という単純な行動には、2つの大きなリスクが伴う。第一に、利回りの高さは「信用
リスク（デフォルトリスク） 」の裏返しである。AIインフラへの投資は巨額であり、すべての企業
がその投資に見合う収益を上げられるとは限らない。競争に敗れた企業や、過剰な債務を抱えた
企業の社債は、元本割れのリスクが高まる。第二に、ファンドを通じた投資に伴う「流動性リス
ク」である。市場環境が良好な時は問題ないが、マクロ経済の悪化やAIブームの調整局面など、
市場にストレスがかかった際には注意が必要である。市場ストレス時にファンドから大規模な資
金流出が生じれば、流動性の低いハイイールド債の売却圧力が強まり、価格下落を増幅させる可
能性がある。 
個人投資家に求められるのは、株式などによるキャピタルゲインに関心が集まりやすいなか、
ポートフォリオにおけるインカムゲインの役割を再評価することである。ただし、それは単に高
利回り商品を買い集めることではない。AI投資の拡大と「金利ある世界」への移行によって広が
る利息収入の機会と、その裏にあるリスクを理解することが重要である。そのうえで、自らのリ
スク許容度や投資期間に合わせて株式と債券のバランスを適切に管理する「インカムゲインの再
設計」が求められる。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
以 上

【注釈】 
1. ハイパースケーラーとは、世界規模で超大型のデータセンター群を保有・運営し、クラウドコ
ンピューティングやAIサービスの基盤を提供する巨大IT企業の総称である。本稿の資料1が対
象とする主要5社は、Bank of Englandの定義に従いAmazon、Microsoft、Alphabet、Meta、
Oracle を指す。

Document 180: AI价格战愈演愈烈，入场壁垒也在下降：高额资本开支还能撑多久？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI价格战愈演愈烈，入场壁垒也在下降：高额资本开支还能撑多久？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

杰富瑞认为，蒸馏技术降低⼤模型⼊场⻔槛，新玩家以数千万美元级融资进⼊头部榜单，
API 价格竞争也随之加剧。尽管 AI 推理需求仍在增⻓、算⼒需求保持扩张，但模型性能差距
收窄和成本下降，也让⾼额资本开⽀能否转化为⾜够回报⾯临更⼤考验。
摘要：
AI ⼤模型竞争正在出现新变化：模型供给持续增加，蒸馏等技术不断降低研发⻔槛，新玩家开始
以远低于头部⼚商的资⾦投⼊进⼊⾼性能模型市场。杰富瑞认为，这⼀趋势正在压低 API 价格，
也让 AI ⾏业此前依赖巨额资本开⽀建⽴竞争壁垒的逻辑⾯临挑战。
据杰富瑞 9 ⽉ 16 ⽇发布的研究报告，在 Artificial Analysis （ AA ）追踪的全球前 14 ⼤语⾔模型榜单
中， 9 ⽉新增 4 个模型，分别来⾃新加坡、韩国、阿联酋和美国。这些模型背后的研发机构融资
规模普遍有限，其中部分机构累计融资仅约 2000 万美元，却已经进⼊全球头部模型榜单。
杰富瑞分析师 Edison Lee 等⼈认为， LLM 市场参与者数量持续增加，各地区对本⼟模型的⽀持

⼒度上升，加上蒸馏技术⼴泛应⽤，正在进⼀步压低⾏业进⼊壁垒。与此同时，模型性能差距
收窄和 API 价格竞争加剧，使 AI 供应链⾯临 “ ⾼资本⽀出、低确定性回报 ” 的新压⼒。
新玩家低成本⼊局，蒸馏技术降低⻔槛
报告显⽰， 9 ⽉新进⼊ AA 前 14 榜单的 4 个模型分别为新加坡 Sapiens AI 的 Agnes 3.0 Flash 、韩国
Motif Technologies 的 Motif-3-Beta 、阿联酋 MBZUAI 的 K2 Horizon ，以及总部位于美国加州、研
发团队在新加坡的 Apodex 1.1 。
其中， Sapiens AI 累计融资约 2000 万美元， Motif Technologies 完成约 1660 万美元 B 轮融资，整
体投⼊规模与头部 AI 企业存在明显差距。
这类案例说明，⼤模型竞争的资本⻔槛正在下降。部分新玩家并没有复制头部企业⼤规模训练
基础模型的路径，⽽是借助开放模型、较⼩规模团队和更⾼效的训练⽅法快速迭代。
蒸馏技术是其中的重要推⼿。通过利⽤现有⼤模型的输出训练新模型，开发者可以⽤更低成本

获得部分先进模型能⼒。杰富瑞认为，这类技术很难被完全封堵，随着应⽤进⼀步普及，后来
者追赶头部模型的成本仍可能下降。
但低成本⼊局并不意味着头部⼚商的优势已经消失。训练最前沿模型仍需要⼤量算⼒、数据和
⼯程投⼊，新玩家更多是在部分能⼒和细分场景中获得竞争⼒。真正的变化在于，“ 进⼊市场 ”
与 “ 做到最强 ” 之间的资本⻔槛正在拉开。
API 价格承压，模型开始进⼊价格竞争
模型数量增加已经开始向 API 价格端传导。杰富瑞数据显⽰，部分新模型的 API 价格已经显著低
于头部模型，其中 Apodex 1.1 的混合 API 价格为每百万 Token 0.3 美元，在 AA 前 15 名模型中属于
价格最低的模型之⼀。
与此同时，头部⼚商并未全⾯加⼊降价，⽽是通过新⼀代模型的能⼒升级寻求更⾼定价。报告
认为，这种提价不仅反映模型能⼒提升，也可能是向资本市场证明商业化能⼒的⼀种⽅式，即
通过更⾼的智能⽔平⽀撑更⾼价格，从⽽改善市场对利润率和投资回报的预期。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
66 收藏 分享到 :
问题在于，涨价与降价正在同时发⽣。头部模型试图⽤性能升级维持溢价，⽽新进⼊者则通过
低成本争夺开发者和应⽤需求。杰富瑞指出，仅美国市场⽬前就已有 7 家⼤模型提供商，参与者
增加意味着 API 市场的价格竞争仍可能持续。
从 “ 拼智能 ” 转向 “ 拼效率 ”
模型竞争的另⼀变化，是⾏业开始从单纯追求模型智能，转向同时⽐拼推理效率。杰富瑞本⽉
引⼊ AutomationBench-AA 基准测试，⽤于衡量模型在智能体⾃动化任务中的能⼒，并据此重新
校准历史数据。
在这⼀背景下，部分模型⼚商开始通过模型架构和内存优化降低推理成本。 DeepSeek 于 9 ⽉ 10
⽇发布 V4.1 Flash 模型，其混合 API 价格降⾄每百万 Token 0.2 美元，较上⼀版本下降 74% 。

该模型还通过 Casual Encoder-Decoder 设计、 Engram 条件记忆机制和 Compressed Sparse
Attention 2 等技术，进⼀步压缩硬件需求： HBM 需求降低约四分之⼀， SSD 需求降低约⼋分之
⼀。杰富瑞认为，这类路线更强调算⼒和内存效率，⽽⾮单纯追求模型智能。
这也意味着， AI 模型的竞争维度正在增加。随着推理 Token 需求快速增⻓，模型⼚商不仅需要提
⾼能⼒，还需要降低每个 Token 的⽣成成本。对于应⽤开发者⽽⾔，如果更低成本的模型已经能
够完成⾜够多的任务，模型之间的价格差异可能⽐单纯的性能差异更加重要。
资本⽀出仍在增⻓，但回报需要重新验证
杰富瑞对 AI 供应链的担忧，核⼼并不是 AI 需求消失，⽽是资本投⼊增⻓速度能否与最终回报匹
配。⼀⽅⾯，新玩家已经能够以数千万美元级别的资⾦投⼊进⼊全球头部模型榜单；另⼀⽅
⾯，头部企业仍在持续扩⼤算⼒、数据中⼼和基础设施投资。
从需求端看， AI 推理需求仍在快速增⻓，模型能⼒提升也会不断催⽣新的应⽤场景，因此算⼒需

求本⾝并未失去增⻓基础。问题在于，当模型供给越来越丰富、 API 价格受到竞争压制后，新增
基础设施投⼊需要多⻓时间才能转化为⾜够的收⼊和利润。
杰富瑞因此认为， AI 产业可能逐渐进⼊⼀个更加注重资本效率的阶段。对于头部企业⽽⾔，继续
扩⼤资本开⽀仍可能是保持技术领先的重要⼿段，但⾏业整体需要更加关注重复建设、资源利
⽤率以及新增算⼒所对应的实际商业价值。
最终，杰富瑞预计，⾏业可能通过整合、合作以及更⾼效的资源配置来降低整体资本开⽀。AI
基础设施需求仍然强劲，但 “ 需求增⻓ ” 并不⾃动等于 “ 所有资本投⼊都能获得⾜够回报 ” ，这将
成为下⼀阶段市场重新审视 AI 估值的重要变量。

Document 193: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, July (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, July (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, July
By Mantas Vanagas
Senior Economist, Westpac
15:35 September 03 2026 Share
The latest goods trade balance surprised to the upside, narrowing only slightly in July. The
details suggest vehicle imports remained strong at the start of Q3, while ADP imports
rebounded, pointing to renewed momentum in data centre investment. Trade balance:
$1.9bn surplus. Export values: -3.3%mth. Import values: -2.5%mth.

### Page 2

This week’s Balance of Payments release highlighted the major themes in Australian international trade
in Q2, while the National Accounts confirmed how these developments affected domestic growth. Net
trade contributed positively to GDP, largely due to an almost 5% drop in services imports. Goods trade
was a drag, however, as the 1.4%qtr increase in exports fell short of the 2.4%qtr rise in imports. Strong
EV sales were reflected in a 38% jump in car import volumes. Meanwhile, the payback in data centre

investment growth following the surge in Q1 was illustrated by a 26% decrease in ADP equipment
imports. Today’s goods trade figures provided an early indication of how these themes evolved at the
start of Q3.
The headline goods trade surplus narrowed slightly to $1.9bn in July but remained above our and
consensus expectations. This was down from an upwardly revised surplus of $2.3bn in June. The
relatively small monthly change contrasted with the considerable volatility of recent months, when the

trade balance oscillated between deficit and surplus. Flows of non-monetary gold, the main driver of
this volatility, again changed significantly in July. This time, however, movements in other components
helped to even things between exports and imports.
Exports
Export revenue fell 3.3%mth, following a 7.6%mth decline in May and a 10.1%mth increase in June.
Gold exports were down by about a quarter, and their monthly change more than fully accounted for

the total exports change. Indeed, excluding gold, total exports rose 0.8%mth.

### Page 3

Movements in the other major components were more muted. Exports of the three major commodities
– iron ore, coal, and LNG – rose only 0.4%mth, as an increase of close to 10% in LNG exports offset
declines in iron ore and coal. Additional detail provided by the ABS suggested that the increase in LNG
revenue was mainly due to higher prices, with a much smaller contribution from volumes. Meanwhile,
weakness in iron ore and coal exports largely reflected lower volumes.

Elsewhere, manufactured goods exports were little changed and remained strong by recent standards.
Rural goods exports rose 5.8%mth to their highest level since February, suggesting that the underlying
strength seen in this category last year remains broadly intact. Looking ahead, however, we expect this
momentum to soften, mainly due to lower supply in the Australian agricultural sector.
Imports
Import revenue fell 2.5%mth in July, marking a third consecutive monthly decline. As with exports, non-

monetary gold was a significant driver. Its 31% drop accounted for a big chunk of the overall decline.
Excluding gold, imports were down by about 1.0%mth.
Performance across the major categories was mixed. Consumer goods imports rose 3.5%mth, well
above the average pace recorded in the first half of the year. Most of the increase reflected an
8.2%mth rise in vehicle imports. This suggests that the momentum in EV sales seen in Q2 may again
contribute to household consumption growth this quarter.

### Page 4

Capital goods imports rose 6.7%mth, reversing a decline of a similar magnitude in June. The ADP
category, which captures imports of data processing equipment used in data centres, rose by 50%.
Following steep declines over the previous three months, the latest reading suggests that data centre
investment may accelerate again this quarter after retreating in Q2.
This is broadly consistent with our expectations that: 1) data centre investment has considerable

upside potential over the medium term; 2) about a half of data centre investment will be matched by an
increase in imports; and 3) investment flows may occur in fits and starts, reflecting both volatility in the
data and the lumpy nature of large investment projects.
Intermediate goods imports fell 7.8%mth, the weakest result since late 2023. Fuel imports declined
12.3%mth, extending the downward trend of recent months following the initial surge in oil prices in

March and April after the war in the Middle East began.
Looking ahead
With gold prices having taken a significant step higher in August, volatility in trade flows is likely to
persist in the coming months as gold exports and imports fluctuate. Other major categories may also
continue to record large month-to-month movements. For example, oil prices have risen from just
above $70 in early July to above $95 now, which may place some upward pressure on fuel import

values.

### Page 5

Related articles
Near-term movements in ADP imports are almost impossible to predict, but the medium-term outlook is
clearer. As long as the economy continues to embrace AI, and Australia remains an attractive location
for the data centres needed to support it, ADP imports are likely to remain strong.
These factors, together with the expectation of firmer domestic economic momentum, point to strong
demand for goods imports. At the same time, the outlook for exports of Australia’s major commodities is

neutral at best, while other categories, including rural goods exports, may begin to soften. The recent
appreciation of the AUD is an additional headwind for exports, and we do not expect this effect to
reverse.
Against this backdrop, we continue to expect the goods trade balance to trend lower over the coming
quarters, dragging the overall current account balance down with it.

Document 281: Switching 9 to 5s Job switching has picked up, with Gen Z leading gains and gender gaps narrowing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Switching 9 to 5s Job switching has picked up, with Gen Z leading gains and gender gaps narrowing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Unknown

### Page 1

13016291     1

Economy 
Switching 9 to 5s 
09 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job-switching pay premiums have risen to their highest level in more than three years, indicating employers may be willing to 
pay for workers with specific skills, according to Bank of America deposit account data. Gen Z continue to benefit the most from 
changing jobs, while workers paid by the hour are also making particularly large gains from switching. 
• Women's lead in wage gains from job changes has narrowed as men's switching premiums have accelerated faster, returning the 
gap to roughly 2018 levels. Meanwhile, pay disruption rates have fallen and converged across genders, although men's higher 
overall pay levels mean broader earnings gaps remain. 
• AI adoption is not yet translating into broad-based labor displacement, according to BofA Global Research. Job switching 
remains resilient, and industries with the highest AI usage do not yet show consistently weaker labor demand. If that changes, 
younger workers and women look most exposed.

Pay increase associated with a job change hits highest rate since 2023 
The broader US labor market momentum picked up in August (read more on this in the August Institute Employment Report), and 
Bank of America data suggests worker mobility may be showing early signs of improvement. In July, increases in pay associated 
with a job change reached their highest rate in over three years, according to Bank of America account data (Exhibit 1). 
Importantly, the annual average for these pay premiums has inched upwards after consistent yearly declines. 
While job-switching pay premiums have improved in recent months, they remain below pre-pandemic levels. This suggests 
today’s labor market differs from both the Great Resignation period in 2022 and the tighter labor markets of the late 2010s.  
Exhibit 1: In July, the pay increase associated with a job change reached the highest rate in over three years 
After-tax pay change associated with a job change (%, three-month moving average, monthly, annual average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Jobs with weekly paychecks see accelerated pay bumps for job switchers 
The premium for switching jobs is most notable among workers with weekly paychecks, though this could also be reflective of 
increased overtime (Exhibit 2). Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) employer payroll data indicates that weekly pay schedules are 
disproportionately concentrated in industries with large hourly workforces and variable hours, particularly construction, leisure 
and hospitality, transportation and warehousing, and portions of retail trade. These jobs often skew lower income. In contrast, 
professional, financial, and other predominantly salaried industries are more likely to use biweekly or semi-monthly payroll 
schedules.  
This acceleration among workers paid weekly provides further evidence that One Big Beautiful Bill Act-related changes to 
overtime hours and tax withholdings could be fueling lower-income wage growth and narrowing differences in spending growth 
across income groups (read more on this in August’s Consumer Checkpoint).  
Exhibit 2: For workers with a weekly paycheck who switched jobs, their change in pay was almost 4x that of those who stayed in their job in July 
After-tax pay change for workers with weekly paychecks by type (monthly, three-month moving average, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Gen Z job change rate outpaces other generations 
Looking across generations, Gen Z have continued to see the greatest pay bumps from switching roles, some of which may 
reflect where they are positioned in the wage distribution (Exhibit 3). Younger workers generally begin their careers at lower 
wage levels, creating more opportunities for substantial percentage increases when they switch jobs. As a result, job changes 
can generate larger proportional pay gains than those typically experienced by older workers. 
Encouragingly, the rate at which Gen Z change jobs has grown and accelerated year-over-year (YoY) since February, surpassing 
all other generations for the first time since 2021 (Exhibit 4). This uptick is particularly noteworthy because it comes at a time 
when many broader labor market indicators suggest slower hiring.  
Gen Z's willingness to keep changing jobs may indicate that opportunities remain available for early-career workers even as 
employers become more selective overall. On the other hand, it could also suggest that companies are prioritizing lower-cost 
entry-level hires over replacing more experienced workers.

Women's job-switching premium is growing more slowly than men's 
Women are still getting a bigger raise than men when they switch jobs, but the difference between women’s and men’s pay 
change from a job change is back to 2018 levels (Exhibit 5). And though women used to change jobs at a faster rate, men’s job 
change rate has edged out women’s since 2024, according to Bank of America account data. 
In our view, the narrowing difference does not necessarily indicate weaker labor market outcomes for women. Instead, it appears 
to reflect faster recent acceleration in men's job-switching wage gains. As a result, the job-switching premium has become more 
similar across genders than it was during much of the post-pandemic period. 
However, the shrinking gap here could pose challenges for pay equality because women’s level of pay often starts from a lower 
base compared to men’s (read more on this in March’s Where are women positioned in a “K-shaped” economy?). Since job 
switching is often one of the fastest ways workers increase earnings, differences in job-switching premiums can have enduring 
effects on wage growth over time. 
Exhibit 5: The difference between women’s and men’s pay change from a job change is back to 2018 levels 
After-tax pay change from a job change by gender (annual average year-to-date, % difference)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Gender gap in pay disruptions narrows to near record low 
Similarly, the gap between women’s and men’s pay disruption rate – which we view as a proxy for temporary or permanent labor 
force departures – has narrowed and both rates have moderated since the start of the year (Exhibit 6) (see Methodology - Pay 
Disruption Rate). The labor market has become more equal on this measure, but not because women's disruption rates have 
fallen dramatically. Rather, men's disruption rates have increasingly resembled women's. 
Interestingly, this is against the backdrop of fewer men in the labor force. Since January, data from the BLS indicates men’s 
growth in the civilian labor force aged 25-54 has fallen, whereas women’s has continued to increase (Exhibit 7).  
Exhibit 6: The pay disruption rate for both men and women has 
fallen over the past few months

AI anxiety is not yet evident in job change rate 
Job-switching behavior may be an important early indicator because labor market adjustments often show up in mobility before 
employment. If firms become more cautious about hiring in the wake of AI adoption, workers may first become less willing or 
less able to switch jobs before broader effects emerge in payroll or unemployment data.  
So, the fact that we’re seeing an increase in job switching is a sign of a somewhat tight labor market despite overall payrolls 
slowing.  
Information and finance/insurance show early signs of AI labor demand impact 
Using a measure of AI usage from the Census Bureau's Business Trend and Outlook Survey (BTOS), which asks respondents if 
they've used AI in the last two weeks for any business function, BofA Global Research found two industries worth watching: 
information as well as finance and insurance. Both have high AI usage and have seen labor demand decline over the five-month 
period ending in June (Exhibit 8). 
However, the lack of a clear relationship in the overall picture suggests AI adoption is not yet translating into broad-based labor 
displacement. In many industries, businesses may still be experimenting with AI tools or using them to augment workers rather 
than replace them.  
Exhibit 8: At this time, there is not a clear relationship between AI usage levels and growth in labor demand overall

Women and younger workers face the greatest AI-related labor market risks 
Although there is no firm AI impact yet, women are more exposed to AI than men, largely because they are overrepresented in 
administrative roles with routine, language-based tasks well suited to generative AI automation or augmentation, according to 
the International Labor Organization (read more on this in May’s Adaptability is the new job security: AI and the future of work). 
BLS forecasts confirm this – demand is expected to be limited for occupations such as procurement clerks; legal secretaries and 
administrative assistants; and customer service representatives. 
And specifically in AI-related roles, women made up just over a quarter of new hires in the US last year — compared with half of 
new hires in non-AI occupations — according to data from a LinkedIn study.1 This suggests that women could be more 
susceptible to the possible negative impact of AI on jobs in more ways than one. 
Younger workers also look potentially exposed because they are more likely to hold roles with automatable tasks and where AI 
adoption is occurring more rapidly (read more on Gen Z’s labor market position in August’s Gen Z reality check). Still, certain 
roles with very high AI exposure2 for recent graduates are expected to have strong growth over the next ten years, according to 
BLS employment occupation projections (Exhibit 9).

1 LinkedIn. (Aug 18, 2026). New LinkedIn Research Finds Women Account for Just 26% of AI Hires as AI Jobs Surge. LinkedIn. 
2 AI exposure indicates potential task transformation – not job loss.
